Jobs health (system tables, direct): the queries

13 queries, each reading the Databricks system tables live. Generated from the dashboard's own SQL; regenerate rather than edit.

Purpose Joins Example Map Basics Rules Limits Checks How to read System tables Widgets Job runs per day: runs, failed and slow, per job Jobs: runs, failures, run time and reliability, one row per job Top 20 failed jobs Failed job runs: $ of runs that failed, per job Jobs slower than usual: the last days against the rest of the window Cold starts: time spent starting clusters, per job Failed cluster starts: runs that failed before any work ran Jobs with no timeout Tasks with no timeout (and none on their job) Jobs with an ownership risk Oversized jobs: compute that sat mostly idle Compute pressure: what limits each job Pipelines: spend per pipeline

What this dashboard is for

Shows which jobs fail, fail now and then (flaky), run slower than usual or wait long for their cluster; which have no timeout or run as a person; which use the wrong size of compute; and what each pipeline costs. Each page answers one of those questions.

How the tables join

Grey: system tables. Blue border: a shared check (its steps are listed under Steps). White: steps of the query. Green: the rows the dashboard shows. ◆ marks the columns the joins use.

  1. lakeflow.job_run_timeline → runs: GROUP BY job_id, run_id
  2. runs → usual: successful runs: median
  3. runs → per job: GROUP BY job_id
  4. usual → per job: JOIN ON job_id
  5. lakeflow.jobs → dashboard rows: JOIN ON job_id (latest row)
  6. access.workspaces_latest → dashboard rows: JOIN ON workspace_id

How it works, with an example

What happens in Databricks, what the system tables record, and how the dashboard's queries read it. Names and ids are made up; table rows are simplified.

1. One job run, minute by minute

The job nightly_sales_load (id 1234) runs at 02:00 on a job cluster. It has two tasks: extract, then load. The load task fails; someone repairs the run, which starts a new cluster and re-runs only the failed task, and this time it succeeds.

queued cluster starting running succeeded failed

  • Queued: waiting for a free slot (the job's concurrency limit). Starting: the cluster's machines boot and install libraries; this is the cold start. Running: the tasks do their work.
  • The run took 110 minutes in total, of which 7 were cluster starts and 5 were idle between the failure and the repair.

2. What each system table records about it

Databricks writes the run into several system tables, each from its own angle. The rows below are simplified (fewer columns); the real tables have more.

system.lakeflow.job_run_timeline: one row per run per hour; only an attempt's last row has a result

job_idrun_idperiod_start_timeperiod_end_timeresult_statetermination_code
1234471102:0003:00
1234471103:0003:15FAILEDRUN_EXECUTION_ERROR
1234471103:2003:50SUCCEEDEDSUCCESS

system.lakeflow.job_task_run_timeline: one row per task run, with its setup and run seconds

task_keyresult_statesetup_duration_secondsexecution_duration_secondscompute_ids
extractSUCCEEDED2402100[0420-cluster-a]
loadFAILED02100[0420-cluster-a]
loadSUCCEEDED1801620[0420-cluster-b]

system.compute.node_timeline: one row per machine per minute, with CPU and memory

cluster_idinstance_iddriverstart_timecpu_user_percentmem_used_percent
0420-cluster-ai-01true02:061235
0420-cluster-ai-02false02:067162
..................

system.billing.usage: DBUs per hour, tagged with the job and the run

usage_start_timesku_nameusage_quantity (DBUs)usage_metadata.job_idusage_metadata.job_run_id
02:00JOBS_COMPUTE14.212344711
03:00JOBS_COMPUTE9.812344711

system.lakeflow.jobs: the job's settings, a new row each time they change

job_idnametimeout_secondsrun_as_user_namechange_time
1234nightly_sales_load0alice@example.com2026-09-01

3. How the dashboard reads it

  • Failed jobs: run 4711 is not a failed run, because its last attempt succeeded. It is a repaired run, and the $ of the failed attempt (from billing.usage) counts in Cost of failed runs.
  • Slow jobs: 110 minutes is compared with the job's usual (median) time. If it usually takes 60, this run is slow (over 1.5x and 5+ minutes longer).
  • Cold starts: the setup seconds (240 and 180) feed the p95 cluster start.
  • Hygiene: timeout_seconds is 0, so the job has no timeout; it runs as alice@example.com, a person, and it is scheduled: an ownership risk.
  • Job size: node_timeline shows how busy the machines were; a worker at 15% CPU the whole run would make the job oversized.

How it fits together

Left: the system tables it reads. Middle: its queries. Right: the pages whose widgets read them.

Databricks basics

TermWhat it means
WorkspaceOne Databricks environment (its own URL, users, jobs, clusters). An account holds many workspaces; the dashboards read all of them at once.
System tablesRead-only tables Databricks fills for the whole account under the catalog system (system.billing, system.lakeflow, system.compute, system.access). The dashboards read them live; there are no tables of our own.
Job, run, taskA job is a scheduled or triggered workflow. Each time it starts that is a run; a run has one or more tasks (a notebook, a SQL file, a pipeline, ...). A failed run can be repaired: re-run in place, which adds an attempt to the same run.
Cluster, driver, workerClassic compute: machines (nodes) Databricks starts for you. The driver coordinates, workers do the work. A job cluster lives for one run; an all-purpose cluster is shared and costs more per DBU. Serverless compute has no cluster you manage.
Cluster start (setup)Before a task can run on a classic cluster the machines must start and install libraries: that wait is the setup time, also called a cold start.
DBU and $Databricks bills in DBUs (Databricks Units). DBUs x the list price of the product (SKU) gives $ at list price; the invoice is usually lower after discounts.
PipelineA Lakeflow Declarative Pipeline (formerly Delta Live Tables): tables kept up to date by updates, either triggered or running continuously.
Median, p95The median is the middle value: half the runs took less, half more; one very long run doesn't move it. p95: 95% of values are at or below it, so it shows the slow tail.
Query, CTEA query is SQL. Each one here is a chain of named steps written WITH name AS (...), called CTEs; each step reads the ones before it, and one last SELECT gives the rows.

Rules and thresholds

RuleDefinitionWhy
Failed runIts last attempt ended FAILED, ERROR or TIMED_OUT.A repaired run that finally succeeded is not a failure.
Always fails3+ runs and every one failed.Broken, not flaky: fix it or switch it off.
Flaky5+ runs and 20%+ of them failed, but not all.Fails often enough to matter, with enough runs to be sure.
Usual run timeThe median of the job's successful runs, once it has 5+.The median ignores the odd very long or short run.
Slow runSucceeded, over 1.5x the usual time and 5+ minutes longer.The extra minutes stop 2-minute jobs being called slow over seconds.
Slower than usualMedian of the last days (up to 7, at most half the window) vs the rest: 1.5x is Warn, 2x Critical; needs 3+ runs on each side.A lasting slowdown, not one bad run.
Slow cold startp95 cluster start 60 s+ is Warn, 300 s+ Critical.A minute of start time on every run adds up.
Failed cluster startA task run that failed in setup with no run time.The cluster never came up; the code never ran.
No timeoutTimeout empty or 0. Warn; Critical when the job spent $200+ in the window.The cost decides how urgent it is.
Ownership riskRuns as a person and is scheduled: Critical. Runs as a person, or creator or run-as missing: Warn.Scheduled work should not depend on one person.
OversizedWorker CPU p90 under 30%, memory peak under 40%, no swap, over 3+ measured runs. Saving: half the $ when under half those limits, else a quarter; $25+ Warn, $200+ Critical.Peak memory, not average, so a smaller node still holds the busiest minute.
Compute pressureMemory p90 85%+ or swap 10%+; skew 40%+ CPU gap between workers; CPU p50 70%+; I/O wait 20%+; driver busy 70%+ while workers idle; idle under 20% CPU.Checked in that order: the first match is the pressure.
Pipeline status2,000+ DBUs and nothing delivered: Critical; 2,000+ DBUs, or 500+ and nothing delivered: Warn.Spend with no completed update is the waste signal.

What it can miss

  • System tables are not real time: job and billing rows can arrive hours late, so today and yesterday read low.
  • Some system tables only hold workspaces in the same cloud region as the metastore; if a workspace is missing, check its region.
  • Only runs that started in the Time Window count; a run that started before it is cut at its start.
  • Job size and compute pressure need node telemetry (system.compute.node_timeline): jobs on serverless or SQL warehouses read Not judged.
  • $ is list price from system.billing.list_prices, not the invoice.
  • A query used on two pages is tagged with the first page's tab in system.query.history.

Check it against your data

Run these in a SQL editor to see the raw rows the dashboard starts from.

A job's runs match the Jobs UI

SELECT run_id, result_state, period_start_time, period_end_time
FROM system.lakeflow.job_run_timeline
WHERE job_id = '<job id>' AND result_state IS NOT NULL
ORDER BY period_end_time DESC LIMIT 20;

Node telemetry exists for a job's cluster

SELECT cluster_id, COUNT(*) AS minutes
FROM system.compute.node_timeline
WHERE start_time >= current_date() - 7
GROUP BY cluster_id ORDER BY minutes DESC LIMIT 20;

How to read a query

  • No snapshot. Each query reads the Databricks system tables directly, every time the dashboard opens or a filter changes.
  • Widgets share queries. Each widget reads one query and adds up its rows its own way (the Widgets table says how); widgets that read the same query always agree.
  • Each query reads like a recipe: named steps (WITH step AS (...), called CTEs), each building on the one before, then one final SELECT. A step named after a check (for example lakeflow_job_reliability) is a shared, tested check reused unchanged except for the dates; its own steps are listed under it.
  • Time Window: :time_range.min and :time_range.max are the dates picked in the Time Window filter (on Global Filters, or on the page). The end day counts in full.
  • Workspace and the other field filters need no SQL: Databricks keeps the rows whose column matches the pick.
  • $ is list price (usage x the SKU's list price), not the invoice.
  • Comments in the SQL (green, starting --) are added on this page only.

System tables

Databricks fills them for the whole account.

System tableWhat it holds
system.access.workspaces_latestEach workspace's id and name.
system.billing.list_pricesThe list price of each SKU over time; DBUs x price = $.
system.billing.usageEvery billed usage record: SKU, DBUs, the day, and the warehouse, cluster, job or endpoint it was for.
system.compute.clustersEach cluster's settings over time: node type, workers, auto-termination.
system.compute.node_timelinePer cluster node, per minute: CPU and memory in use.
system.compute.node_typesEach machine type: memory and cores.
system.lakeflow.job_run_timelineEach job run: start, end and result (succeeded, failed, ...).
system.lakeflow.job_task_run_timelineEach task inside a job run, with the compute it ran on.
system.lakeflow.job_tasksEach task of each job and its settings (timeout, ...), over time.
system.lakeflow.jobsEach job's name and settings.
system.lakeflow.pipeline_update_timelineEach pipeline update: start, end and result.
system.lakeflow.pipelinesEach Lakeflow (DLT) pipeline's name and settings.

The queries

Job runs per day: runs, failed and slow, per job

Shows: Every job's runs per day: how many, how many failed, how many were slow. Used by: The charts over time on Failed jobs and Failed job details.

Why it is built this way: Counting rows in job_run_timeline would count a long run several times (it writes one row per hour) and a repaired run once per attempt. So the query keeps one row per run, takes the result of its last attempt, and only then counts per day. Slow runs are judged against the job's own usual time, because 20 minutes is normal for one job and alarming for another.

How its tables join

Grey: system tables. Blue border: a shared check (its steps are listed under Steps). White: steps of the query. Green: the rows the dashboard shows. ◆ marks the columns the joins use.

  1. lakeflow.job_run_timeline → ends: rows with a result; the last per run (QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER)
  2. lakeflow.job_run_timeline → starts: MIN(period_start_time) per run
  3. ends → runs: JOIN ON workspace_id, job_id, run_id
  4. starts → runs: JOIN ON workspace_id, job_id, run_id
  5. runs → usual: successful runs: median per job, 5+ runs
  6. runs → judged: every run
  7. usual → judged: LEFT JOIN ON workspace_id, job_id; slow = 1.5x and 5+ min
  8. judged → named: every run
  9. jb → named: LEFT JOIN ON workspace_id, job_id
  10. ws → named: LEFT JOIN ON workspace_id
  11. named → ranked: SUM(failed) per job; DENSE_RANK
  12. ranked → dashboard rows: top 10 keep their name; GROUP BY day and job

1. System tables it reads

  • system.access.workspaces_latest
  • system.lakeflow.job_run_timeline
  • system.lakeflow.jobs

2. Steps (CTEs), in order

  1. ws: Workspace id to name.
    Its SQL (6 lines)
    -- Job runs per day: runs, failed and slow, per job. List price; dates from the Time Window.
-- Workspace id to name.
ws AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, workspace_name
  FROM system.access.workspaces_latest
)
  2. jb: Job id to name: the latest row per job.
    Its SQL (6 lines)
    -- Job id to name: the latest row per job.
jb AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, CAST(job_id AS STRING) AS job_id, name
  FROM system.lakeflow.jobs
  QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY workspace_id, job_id ORDER BY change_time DESC) = 1
)
  3. slices: Job run rows that started in the window; a long run has one row per hour.

    What it brings, on the example:

    job_idrun_idperiod_start_timeperiod_end_timeresult_state
    1234471102:0003:00(empty)
    1234471103:0003:15FAILED
    1234471103:2003:50SUCCEEDED
    Its SQL (7 lines)
    -- Job run rows that started in the window; a long run has one row per hour.
slices AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, CAST(job_id AS STRING) AS job_id, run_id,
         period_start_time, period_end_time, result_state, termination_code, run_duration_seconds
  FROM system.lakeflow.job_run_timeline
  WHERE period_start_time >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) AND period_start_time < date_add(:time_range.max, 1)
)
  4. ends: Each run's end row; a repaired run keeps its last attempt's result and error.

    What it brings, on the example:

    job_idrun_idrun_endresult_statetermination_code
    1234471103:50SUCCEEDEDSUCCESS
    Its SQL (9 lines)
    -- Each run's end row; a repaired run keeps its last attempt's result and error.
ends AS (
  -- a repaired run writes one end row per attempt: its result is the last one's
  SELECT workspace_id, job_id, run_id, period_end_time AS run_end, result_state, termination_code,
         NULLIF(run_duration_seconds, 0) AS reported_s
  FROM slices
  WHERE result_state IS NOT NULL
  QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY workspace_id, job_id, run_id ORDER BY period_end_time DESC) = 1
)
  5. starts: Each run's first row: when it started.

    What it brings, on the example:

    job_idrun_idrun_start
    1234471102:00
    Its SQL (6 lines)
    -- Each run's first row: when it started.
starts AS (
  SELECT workspace_id, job_id, run_id, MIN(period_start_time) AS run_start
  FROM slices
  GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id, run_id
)
  6. runs: One row per run: day, minutes it took, and 1 when it failed (failed, error or timed out).

    What it brings, on the example:

    job_idrun_idrun_dateminutesfailed
    123447112026-10-201100
    123446902026-10-19580
    123446552026-10-17411
    Its SQL (9 lines)
    -- One row per run: day, minutes it took, and 1 when it failed (failed, error or timed out).
runs AS (
  SELECT e.workspace_id, e.job_id, e.run_id, e.run_end, e.result_state, e.termination_code,
         CAST(s.run_start AS DATE) AS run_date,
         COALESCE(e.reported_s, timestampdiff(SECOND, s.run_start, e.run_end)) / 60.0 AS minutes,
         CASE WHEN e.result_state IN ('FAILED', 'ERROR', 'TIMED_OUT') THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS failed
  FROM ends e
  JOIN starts s ON s.workspace_id = e.workspace_id AND s.job_id = e.job_id AND s.run_id = e.run_id
)
  7. usual: Each job's usual run time: the median of its successful runs, once it has 5 or more.

    What it brings, on the example:

    job_idusual_minutes
    123460
    Its SQL (8 lines)
    -- Each job's usual run time: the median of its successful runs, once it has 5 or more.
usual AS (
  SELECT workspace_id, job_id, percentile(minutes, 0.5) AS usual_minutes
  FROM runs
  WHERE result_state = 'SUCCEEDED'
  GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id
  HAVING COUNT(*) >= 5
)
  8. judged: A slow run: succeeded, but took over 1.5x the job's usual time and 5+ minutes longer.

    What it brings, on the example:

    run_idminutesusual_minutesslow
    4711110601 (110 > 1.5 x 60 and 50 min longer)
    469058600
    465541600 (failed runs are never slow)
    Its SQL (8 lines)
    -- A slow run: succeeded, but took over 1.5x the job's usual time and 5+ minutes longer.
judged AS (
  SELECT r.*, u.usual_minutes,
         CASE WHEN r.result_state = 'SUCCEEDED' AND r.minutes > 1.5 * u.usual_minutes
                   AND r.minutes - u.usual_minutes >= 5 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS slow
  FROM runs r
  LEFT JOIN usual u ON u.workspace_id = r.workspace_id AND u.job_id = r.job_id
)
  9. named: Workspace and job names added.

    What it brings, on the example:

    run_idjob_nameworkspace
    4711nightly_sales_loadanalytics-prod
    Its SQL (7 lines)
    -- Workspace and job names added.
named AS (
  SELECT j.*, COALESCE(ws.workspace_name, CAST(j.workspace_id AS STRING), 'Account-level') AS workspace, COALESCE(jb.name, j.job_id) AS job_name
  FROM judged j
  LEFT JOIN ws ON ws.workspace_id = j.workspace_id
  LEFT JOIN jb ON jb.workspace_id = j.workspace_id AND jb.job_id = j.job_id
)
  10. ranked: Jobs ranked by their failed runs in the window.

    What it brings, on the example:

    job_namejob_failedrk
    nightly_sales_load25
    Its SQL (5 lines)
    -- Jobs ranked by their failed runs in the window.
ranked AS (
  SELECT *, DENSE_RANK() OVER (ORDER BY job_failed DESC, workspace_id, job_id) AS rk
  FROM (SELECT *, SUM(failed) OVER (PARTITION BY workspace_id, job_id) AS job_failed FROM named)
)
  11. labelled: The 10 with the most failed runs keep their name; the rest read All others.

    What it brings, on the example:

    run_datejobtop_failing_jobfailedslow
    2026-10-20nightly_sales_load (1234)nightly_sales_load · analytics-prod01
    Its SQL (7 lines)
    -- The 10 with the most failed runs keep their name; the rest read All others.
labelled AS (
  SELECT run_date, workspace, concat(job_name, ' (', job_id, ')') AS job,
         CASE WHEN job_failed > 0 AND rk <= 10 THEN concat(job_name, ' · ', workspace) ELSE 'All others' END AS top_failing_job,
         failed, slow
  FROM ranked
)

3. Final query

One row per job and day; the 10 jobs with the most failed runs keep their name in top_failing_job.

4. Columns it returns

ColumnMeaning
run_dateThe day the run started.
workspaceThe workspace's name (its id when the name is unknown).
jobThe job's name and id, as name (id). One-off submitted runs have no name and show the id.
top_failing_jobThe job's name for the 10 jobs with the most failed runs in the window; All others for the rest.
runsRuns that ended that day (one per run, repairs not counted twice).
failed_runsRuns whose last attempt ended FAILED, ERROR or TIMED_OUT.
slow_runsSuccessful runs that were slow by the slow-run rule.
SQL with comments (72 lines)
-- Job runs per day: runs, failed and slow, per job. List price; dates from the Time Window.
-- Workspace id to name.
WITH ws AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, workspace_name
  FROM system.access.workspaces_latest
),
-- Job id to name: the latest row per job.
jb AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, CAST(job_id AS STRING) AS job_id, name
  FROM system.lakeflow.jobs
  QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY workspace_id, job_id ORDER BY change_time DESC) = 1
),
-- Job run rows that started in the window; a long run has one row per hour.
slices AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, CAST(job_id AS STRING) AS job_id, run_id,
         period_start_time, period_end_time, result_state, termination_code, run_duration_seconds
  FROM system.lakeflow.job_run_timeline
  WHERE period_start_time >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) AND period_start_time < date_add(:time_range.max, 1)
),
-- Each run's end row; a repaired run keeps its last attempt's result and error.
ends AS (
  -- a repaired run writes one end row per attempt: its result is the last one's
  SELECT workspace_id, job_id, run_id, period_end_time AS run_end, result_state, termination_code,
         NULLIF(run_duration_seconds, 0) AS reported_s
  FROM slices
  WHERE result_state IS NOT NULL
  QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY workspace_id, job_id, run_id ORDER BY period_end_time DESC) = 1
),
-- Each run's first row: when it started.
starts AS (
  SELECT workspace_id, job_id, run_id, MIN(period_start_time) AS run_start
  FROM slices
  GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id, run_id
),
-- One row per run: day, minutes it took, and 1 when it failed (failed, error or timed out).
runs AS (
  SELECT e.workspace_id, e.job_id, e.run_id, e.run_end, e.result_state, e.termination_code,
         CAST(s.run_start AS DATE) AS run_date,
         COALESCE(e.reported_s, timestampdiff(SECOND, s.run_start, e.run_end)) / 60.0 AS minutes,
         CASE WHEN e.result_state IN ('FAILED', 'ERROR', 'TIMED_OUT') THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS failed
  FROM ends e
  JOIN starts s ON s.workspace_id = e.workspace_id AND s.job_id = e.job_id AND s.run_id = e.run_id
),
-- Each job's usual run time: the median of its successful runs, once it has 5 or more.
usual AS (
  SELECT workspace_id, job_id, percentile(minutes, 0.5) AS usual_minutes
  FROM runs
  WHERE result_state = 'SUCCEEDED'
  GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id
  HAVING COUNT(*) >= 5
),
-- A slow run: succeeded, but took over 1.5x the job's usual time and 5+ minutes longer.
judged AS (
  SELECT r.*, u.usual_minutes,
         CASE WHEN r.result_state = 'SUCCEEDED' AND r.minutes > 1.5 * u.usual_minutes
                   AND r.minutes - u.usual_minutes >= 5 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS slow
  FROM runs r
  LEFT JOIN usual u ON u.workspace_id = r.workspace_id AND u.job_id = r.job_id
),
-- Workspace and job names added.
named AS (
  SELECT j.*, COALESCE(ws.workspace_name, CAST(j.workspace_id AS STRING), 'Account-level') AS workspace, COALESCE(jb.name, j.job_id) AS job_name
  FROM judged j
  LEFT JOIN ws ON ws.workspace_id = j.workspace_id
  LEFT JOIN jb ON jb.workspace_id = j.workspace_id AND jb.job_id = j.job_id
),
-- Jobs ranked by their failed runs in the window.
ranked AS (
  SELECT *, DENSE_RANK() OVER (ORDER BY job_failed DESC, workspace_id, job_id) AS rk
  FROM (SELECT *, SUM(failed) OVER (PARTITION BY workspace_id, job_id) AS job_failed FROM named)
),
-- The 10 with the most failed runs keep their name; the rest read All others.
labelled AS (
  SELECT run_date, workspace, concat(job_name, ' (', job_id, ')') AS job,
         CASE WHEN job_failed > 0 AND rk <= 10 THEN concat(job_name, ' · ', workspace) ELSE 'All others' END AS top_failing_job,
         failed, slow
  FROM ranked
)
-- Final SELECT: the rows the widgets read.
SELECT run_date, workspace, job, top_failing_job,
       COUNT(*) AS runs, SUM(failed) AS failed_runs, SUM(slow) AS slow_runs
FROM labelled
GROUP BY run_date, workspace, job, top_failing_job
ORDER BY run_date, workspace, job

Jobs: runs, failures, run time and reliability, one row per job

Shows: Per job that ran: runs, failures, failure %, days with failures or slow runs, run times, and its reliability. Used by: The tiles and reliability table on Failed jobs, and the run-time chart and table on Slow jobs.

Why it is built this way: One row per job lets every tile, chart and table on two pages read the same numbers, so they always agree. Reliability needs a minimum number of runs: one failure in two runs is not a pattern.

How its tables join

Grey: system tables. Blue border: a shared check (its steps are listed under Steps). White: steps of the query. Green: the rows the dashboard shows. ◆ marks the columns the joins use.

  1. lakeflow.job_run_timeline → ends: rows with a result; the last per run (QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER)
  2. lakeflow.job_run_timeline → starts: MIN(period_start_time) per run
  3. ends → runs: JOIN ON workspace_id, job_id, run_id
  4. starts → runs: JOIN ON workspace_id, job_id, run_id
  5. runs → usual: successful runs: median per job, 5+ runs
  6. runs → judged: every run
  7. usual → judged: LEFT JOIN ON workspace_id, job_id; slow = 1.5x and 5+ min
  8. judged → per_job: GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id
  9. jb → named: LEFT JOIN ON workspace_id, job_id (latest row)
  10. ws → named: LEFT JOIN ON workspace_id
  11. named → ranked: reliability rule; ROW_NUMBER by failed runs and by hours
  12. ranked → dashboard rows: all jobs, always failing and flaky first

1. System tables it reads

  • system.access.workspaces_latest
  • system.lakeflow.job_run_timeline
  • system.lakeflow.jobs

    What it brings, on the example:

    job_idrun_idrun_endresult_statetermination_code

    Its SQL (9 lines)
    -- Each run's end row; a repaired run keeps its last attempt's result and error.
ends AS (
  -- a repaired run writes one end row per attempt: its result is the last one's

         NULLIF(run_duration_seconds, 0) AS reported_s

  5. starts: Each run's first row: when it started.

    What it brings, on the example:

    job_idrun_idrun_start
    1234471102:00
    Its SQL (6 lines)
    -- Each run's first row: when it started.
starts AS (
  SELECT workspace_id, job_id, run_id, MIN(period_start_time) AS run_start
  FROM slices
  GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id, run_id
)
  6. runs: One row per run: day, minutes it took, and 1 when it failed (failed, error or timed out).

    What it brings, on the example:

    job_idrun_idrun_dateminutesfailed
    123447112026-10-201100
    123446902026-10-19580
    123446552026-10-17411
    Its SQL (9 lines)
    -- One row per run: day, minutes it took, and 1 when it failed (failed, error or timed out).
runs AS (
  SELECT e.workspace_id, e.job_id, e.run_id, e.run_end, e.result_state, e.termination_code,
         CAST(s.run_start AS DATE) AS run_date,
         COALESCE(e.reported_s, timestampdiff(SECOND, s.run_start, e.run_end)) / 60.0 AS minutes,
         CASE WHEN e.result_state IN ('FAILED', 'ERROR', 'TIMED_OUT') THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS failed
  FROM ends e
  JOIN starts s ON s.workspace_id = e.workspace_id AND s.job_id = e.job_id AND s.run_id = e.run_id
)
  7. usual: Each job's usual run time: the median of its successful runs, once it has 5 or more.

    What it brings, on the example:

    job_idusual_minutes
    123460
    Its SQL (8 lines)
    -- Each job's usual run time: the median of its successful runs, once it has 5 or more.
usual AS (
  SELECT workspace_id, job_id, percentile(minutes, 0.5) AS usual_minutes
  FROM runs
  WHERE result_state = 'SUCCEEDED'
  GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id
  HAVING COUNT(*) >= 5
)
  8. judged: A slow run: succeeded, but took over 1.5x the job's usual time and 5+ minutes longer.

    What it brings, on the example:

    run_idminutesusual_minutesslow
    4711110601 (110 > 1.5 x 60 and 50 min longer)
    469058600
    465541600 (failed runs are never slow)
    Its SQL (8 lines)
    -- A slow run: succeeded, but took over 1.5x the job's usual time and 5+ minutes longer.
judged AS (
  SELECT r.*, u.usual_minutes,
         CASE WHEN r.result_state = 'SUCCEEDED' AND r.minutes > 1.5 * u.usual_minutes
                   AND r.minutes - u.usual_minutes >= 5 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS slow
  FROM runs r
  LEFT JOIN usual u ON u.workspace_id = r.workspace_id AND u.job_id = r.job_id
)
  9. per_job: Per job: runs, failed and slow runs, days with a failure or a slow run, median, average and longest run, total hours, and the latest failure's error.

    What it brings, on the example:

    job_idrunsfailed_runsslow_runsdays_failedmedian_minutesmax_minuteslast_code
    12343023261110RUN_EXECUTION_ERROR
    Its SQL (12 lines)
    -- Per job: runs, failed and slow runs, days with a failure or a slow run, median, average and longest run, total hours, and the latest failure's error.
per_job AS (
  SELECT workspace_id, job_id, COUNT(*) AS runs, SUM(failed) AS failed_runs, SUM(slow) AS slow_runs,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN failed = 1 THEN run_date END) AS days_failed,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN slow = 1 THEN run_date END) AS days_slow,
         percentile(minutes, 0.5) AS median_minutes, AVG(minutes) AS avg_minutes, MAX(minutes) AS max_minutes,
         SUM(minutes) / 60.0 AS total_hours,
         -- the error of its latest failed run
         MAX_BY(termination_code, CASE WHEN failed = 1 THEN unix_timestamp(run_end) ELSE 0 END) AS last_code
  FROM judged
  GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id
)
  10. named: Workspace and job names added.

    What it brings, on the example:

    job_idjob_nameworkspace
    1234nightly_sales_loadanalytics-prod
    Its SQL (7 lines)
    -- Workspace and job names added.
named AS (
  SELECT j.*, COALESCE(ws.workspace_name, CAST(j.workspace_id AS STRING), 'Account-level') AS workspace, COALESCE(jb.name, j.job_id) AS job_name
  FROM per_job j
  LEFT JOIN ws ON ws.workspace_id = j.workspace_id
  LEFT JOIN jb ON jb.workspace_id = j.workspace_id AND jb.job_id = j.job_id
)
  11. ranked: Reliability: Always fails (every run, 3+), Flaky (5+ runs, 20%+ failed), Some failures, No failures; ranked by failed runs and by total hours.

    What it brings, on the example:

    job_namereliabilityfail_ranktime_rank
    nightly_sales_loadSome failures (2 of 30 = 7%, under 20%)52
    Its SQL (7 lines)
    -- Reliability: Always fails (every run, 3+), Flaky (5+ runs, 20%+ failed), Some failures, No failures; ranked by failed runs and by total hours.
ranked AS (
  SELECT *, CASE WHEN failed_runs = 0 THEN 'No failures' WHEN failed_runs = runs AND runs >= 3 THEN 'Always fails' WHEN runs >= 5 AND 100.0 * failed_runs / runs >= 20 THEN 'Flaky' ELSE 'Some failures' END AS reliability,
         ROW_NUMBER() OVER (ORDER BY failed_runs DESC, failed_runs / runs DESC, workspace, job_name) AS fail_rank,
         ROW_NUMBER() OVER (ORDER BY total_hours DESC, workspace, job_name) AS time_rank
  FROM named
)

3. Final query

One row per job, always failing and flaky first.

4. Columns it returns

ColumnMeaning
jobThe job's name and id, as name (id). One-off submitted runs have no name and show the id.
workspaceThe workspace's name (its id when the name is unknown).
reliabilityAlways fails, Flaky, Some failures or No failures (see Rules).
jobsAlways 1: charts add it up to count jobs.
runsRuns in the window (one per run).
failed_runsRuns whose last attempt failed, errored or timed out.
failure_pctfailed_runs / runs x 100.
days_failedDays with at least one failed run.
days_slowDays with at least one slow run.
slow_runsSuccessful runs that took over 1.5x the job's usual time and 5+ minutes longer.
median_minutesThe middle run time of all its runs, in minutes.
avg_minutesThe average run time, in minutes.
max_minutesThe longest run, in minutes.
total_hoursAll its run time added up, in hours.
last_failureThe error code of its latest failed run (empty when none failed).
failing1 when the job had any failed run.
flaky1 when reliability is Flaky.
always_fails1 when reliability is Always fails.
top_by_timeThe job's name for the 10 jobs with the most run time; All others for the rest.
SQL with comments (85 lines)

-- Workspace id to name.
WITH ws AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, workspace_name
  FROM system.access.workspaces_latest
),
-- Job id to name: the latest row per job.
jb AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, CAST(job_id AS STRING) AS job_id, name
  FROM system.lakeflow.jobs
  QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY workspace_id, job_id ORDER BY change_time DESC) = 1
),
-- Job run rows that started in the window; a long run has one row per hour.
slices AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, CAST(job_id AS STRING) AS job_id, run_id,
         period_start_time, period_end_time, result_state, termination_code, run_duration_seconds
  FROM system.lakeflow.job_run_timeline
  WHERE period_start_time >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) AND period_start_time < date_add(:time_range.max, 1)
),
-- Each run's end row; a repaired run keeps its last attempt's result and error.
ends AS (
  -- a repaired run writes one end row per attempt: its result is the last one's
  SELECT workspace_id, job_id, run_id, period_end_time AS run_end, result_state, termination_code,
         NULLIF(run_duration_seconds, 0) AS reported_s
  FROM slices
  WHERE result_state IS NOT NULL
  QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY workspace_id, job_id, run_id ORDER BY period_end_time DESC) = 1
),
-- Each run's first row: when it started.
starts AS (
  SELECT workspace_id, job_id, run_id, MIN(period_start_time) AS run_start
  FROM slices
  GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id, run_id
),
-- One row per run: day, minutes it took, and 1 when it failed (failed, error or timed out).
runs AS (
  SELECT e.workspace_id, e.job_id, e.run_id, e.run_end, e.result_state, e.termination_code,
         CAST(s.run_start AS DATE) AS run_date,
         COALESCE(e.reported_s, timestampdiff(SECOND, s.run_start, e.run_end)) / 60.0 AS minutes,
         CASE WHEN e.result_state IN ('FAILED', 'ERROR', 'TIMED_OUT') THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS failed
  FROM ends e
  JOIN starts s ON s.workspace_id = e.workspace_id AND s.job_id = e.job_id AND s.run_id = e.run_id
),
-- Each job's usual run time: the median of its successful runs, once it has 5 or more.
usual AS (
  SELECT workspace_id, job_id, percentile(minutes, 0.5) AS usual_minutes
  FROM runs
  WHERE result_state = 'SUCCEEDED'
  GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id
  HAVING COUNT(*) >= 5
),
-- A slow run: succeeded, but took over 1.5x the job's usual time and 5+ minutes longer.
judged AS (
  SELECT r.*, u.usual_minutes,
         CASE WHEN r.result_state = 'SUCCEEDED' AND r.minutes > 1.5 * u.usual_minutes
                   AND r.minutes - u.usual_minutes >= 5 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS slow
  FROM runs r
  LEFT JOIN usual u ON u.workspace_id = r.workspace_id AND u.job_id = r.job_id
),
-- Per job: runs, failed and slow runs, days with a failure or a slow run, median, average and longest run, total hours, and the latest failure's error.
per_job AS (
  SELECT workspace_id, job_id, COUNT(*) AS runs, SUM(failed) AS failed_runs, SUM(slow) AS slow_runs,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN failed = 1 THEN run_date END) AS days_failed,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN slow = 1 THEN run_date END) AS days_slow,
         percentile(minutes, 0.5) AS median_minutes, AVG(minutes) AS avg_minutes, MAX(minutes) AS max_minutes,
         SUM(minutes) / 60.0 AS total_hours,
         -- the error of its latest failed run
         MAX_BY(termination_code, CASE WHEN failed = 1 THEN unix_timestamp(run_end) ELSE 0 END) AS last_code
  FROM judged
  GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id
),
-- Workspace and job names added.
named AS (
  SELECT j.*, COALESCE(ws.workspace_name, CAST(j.workspace_id AS STRING), 'Account-level') AS workspace, COALESCE(jb.name, j.job_id) AS job_name
  FROM per_job j
  LEFT JOIN ws ON ws.workspace_id = j.workspace_id
  LEFT JOIN jb ON jb.workspace_id = j.workspace_id AND jb.job_id = j.job_id
),
-- Reliability: Always fails (every run, 3+), Flaky (5+ runs, 20%+ failed), Some failures, No failures; ranked by failed runs and by total hours.
ranked AS (
  SELECT *, CASE WHEN failed_runs = 0 THEN 'No failures' WHEN failed_runs = runs AND runs >= 3 THEN 'Always fails' WHEN runs >= 5 AND 100.0 * failed_runs / runs >= 20 THEN 'Flaky' ELSE 'Some failures' END AS reliability,
         ROW_NUMBER() OVER (ORDER BY failed_runs DESC, failed_runs / runs DESC, workspace, job_name) AS fail_rank,
         ROW_NUMBER() OVER (ORDER BY total_hours DESC, workspace, job_name) AS time_rank
  FROM named
)
-- Final SELECT: the rows the widgets read.
SELECT concat(job_name, ' (', job_id, ')') AS job, workspace, reliability, 1 AS jobs,
       runs, failed_runs, ROUND(100.0 * failed_runs / runs, 1) AS failure_pct, days_failed, days_slow, slow_runs,
       ROUND(median_minutes, 1) AS median_minutes, ROUND(avg_minutes, 1) AS avg_minutes,
       ROUND(max_minutes, 1) AS max_minutes, ROUND(total_hours, 1) AS total_hours,
       CASE WHEN failed_runs > 0 THEN last_code END AS last_failure,
       CASE WHEN failed_runs > 0 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS failing,
       CASE WHEN reliability = 'Flaky' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS flaky,
       CASE WHEN reliability = 'Always fails' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS always_fails,
       CASE WHEN time_rank <= 10 THEN concat(job_name, ' · ', workspace) ELSE 'All others' END AS top_by_time
FROM ranked
ORDER BY CASE reliability WHEN 'Always fails' THEN 0 WHEN 'Flaky' THEN 1 WHEN 'Some failures' THEN 2 ELSE 3 END, failure_pct DESC, total_hours DESC

Top 20 failed jobs

Shows: The 20 jobs with the most failed runs, with their run counts, failure %, median run time and the days they failed or ran slow. Used by: The table on Failed job details.

Why it is built this way: The same per-job numbers as the jobs query, cut to the 20 jobs with the most failed runs, so the details table is short and ordered by what needs attention.

How its tables join

Grey: system tables. Blue border: a shared check (its steps are listed under Steps). White: steps of the query. Green: the rows the dashboard shows. ◆ marks the columns the joins use.

  1. lakeflow.job_run_timeline → ends: rows with a result; the last per run (QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER)
  2. lakeflow.job_run_timeline → starts: MIN(period_start_time) per run
  3. ends → runs: JOIN ON workspace_id, job_id, run_id
  4. starts → runs: JOIN ON workspace_id, job_id, run_id
  5. runs → usual: successful runs: median per job, 5+ runs
  6. runs → judged: every run

  8. judged → per_job: GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id
  9. jb → named: LEFT JOIN ON workspace_id, job_id (latest row)
  10. ws → named: LEFT JOIN ON workspace_id
  11. named → ranked: reliability rule; ROW_NUMBER by failed runs and by hours
  12. ranked → dashboard rows: WHERE failed_runs > 0 AND fail_rank <= 20

    What it brings, on the example:

    job_idrun_idrun_start
    1234471102:00
    Its SQL (6 lines)
    -- Each run's first row: when it started.
starts AS (
  SELECT workspace_id, job_id, run_id, MIN(period_start_time) AS run_start
  FROM slices
  GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id, run_id
)
  6. runs: One row per run: day, minutes it took, and 1 when it failed (failed, error or timed out).

    What it brings, on the example:

    job_idrun_idrun_dateminutesfailed
    123447112026-10-201100
    123446902026-10-19580
    123446552026-10-17411
    Its SQL (9 lines)
    -- One row per run: day, minutes it took, and 1 when it failed (failed, error or timed out).
runs AS (
  SELECT e.workspace_id, e.job_id, e.run_id, e.run_end, e.result_state, e.termination_code,
         CAST(s.run_start AS DATE) AS run_date,
         COALESCE(e.reported_s, timestampdiff(SECOND, s.run_start, e.run_end)) / 60.0 AS minutes,
         CASE WHEN e.result_state IN ('FAILED', 'ERROR', 'TIMED_OUT') THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS failed
  FROM ends e
  JOIN starts s ON s.workspace_id = e.workspace_id AND s.job_id = e.job_id AND s.run_id = e.run_id
)
  7. usual: Each job's usual run time: the median of its successful runs, once it has 5 or more.

    What it brings, on the example:

    job_idusual_minutes
    123460
    Its SQL (8 lines)
    -- Each job's usual run time: the median of its successful runs, once it has 5 or more.
usual AS (
  SELECT workspace_id, job_id, percentile(minutes, 0.5) AS usual_minutes
  FROM runs
  WHERE result_state = 'SUCCEEDED'
  GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id
  HAVING COUNT(*) >= 5
)
  8. judged: A slow run: succeeded, but took over 1.5x the job's usual time and 5+ minutes longer.

    What it brings, on the example:

    run_idminutesusual_minutesslow
    4711110601 (110 > 1.5 x 60 and 50 min longer)
    469058600
    465541600 (failed runs are never slow)
    Its SQL (8 lines)
    -- A slow run: succeeded, but took over 1.5x the job's usual time and 5+ minutes longer.
judged AS (
  SELECT r.*, u.usual_minutes,
         CASE WHEN r.result_state = 'SUCCEEDED' AND r.minutes > 1.5 * u.usual_minutes
                   AND r.minutes - u.usual_minutes >= 5 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS slow
  FROM runs r
  LEFT JOIN usual u ON u.workspace_id = r.workspace_id AND u.job_id = r.job_id
)
  9. per_job: Per job: runs, failed and slow runs, days with a failure or a slow run, median, average and longest run, total hours, and the latest failure's error.

    What it brings, on the example:

    job_idrunsfailed_runsslow_runsdays_failedmedian_minutesmax_minuteslast_code
    12343023261110RUN_EXECUTION_ERROR
    Its SQL (12 lines)
    -- Per job: runs, failed and slow runs, days with a failure or a slow run, median, average and longest run, total hours, and the latest failure's error.
per_job AS (
  SELECT workspace_id, job_id, COUNT(*) AS runs, SUM(failed) AS failed_runs, SUM(slow) AS slow_runs,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN failed = 1 THEN run_date END) AS days_failed,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN slow = 1 THEN run_date END) AS days_slow,
         percentile(minutes, 0.5) AS median_minutes, AVG(minutes) AS avg_minutes, MAX(minutes) AS max_minutes,
         SUM(minutes) / 60.0 AS total_hours,
         -- the error of its latest failed run
         MAX_BY(termination_code, CASE WHEN failed = 1 THEN unix_timestamp(run_end) ELSE 0 END) AS last_code
  FROM judged
  GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id
)
  10. named: Workspace and job names added.

    What it brings, on the example:

    job_idjob_nameworkspace
    1234nightly_sales_loadanalytics-prod
    Its SQL (7 lines)
    -- Workspace and job names added.
named AS (
  SELECT j.*, COALESCE(ws.workspace_name, CAST(j.workspace_id AS STRING), 'Account-level') AS workspace, COALESCE(jb.name, j.job_id) AS job_name
  FROM per_job j
  LEFT JOIN ws ON ws.workspace_id = j.workspace_id
  LEFT JOIN jb ON jb.workspace_id = j.workspace_id AND jb.job_id = j.job_id
)
  11. ranked: Reliability: Always fails (every run, 3+), Flaky (5+ runs, 20%+ failed), Some failures, No failures; ranked by failed runs and by total hours.

    What it brings, on the example:

    job_namereliabilityfail_ranktime_rank
    nightly_sales_loadSome failures (2 of 30 = 7%, under 20%)52
    Its SQL (7 lines)
    -- Reliability: Always fails (every run, 3+), Flaky (5+ runs, 20%+ failed), Some failures, No failures; ranked by failed runs and by total hours.
ranked AS (
  SELECT *, CASE WHEN failed_runs = 0 THEN 'No failures' WHEN failed_runs = runs AND runs >= 3 THEN 'Always fails' WHEN runs >= 5 AND 100.0 * failed_runs / runs >= 20 THEN 'Flaky' ELSE 'Some failures' END AS reliability,
         ROW_NUMBER() OVER (ORDER BY failed_runs DESC, failed_runs / runs DESC, workspace, job_name) AS fail_rank,
         ROW_NUMBER() OVER (ORDER BY total_hours DESC, workspace, job_name) AS time_rank
  FROM named
)

3. Final query

Up to 20 rows, most failed runs first.

4. Columns it returns

ColumnMeaning
jobThe job's name and id, as name (id). One-off submitted runs have no name and show the id.
workspaceThe workspace's name (its id when the name is unknown).
reliabilityAlways fails, Flaky, Some failures or No failures (see Rules).
jobsAlways 1: charts add it up to count jobs.
runsRuns in the window (one per run).
failed_runsRuns whose last attempt failed, errored or timed out.
failure_pctfailed_runs / runs x 100.
days_failedDays with at least one failed run.
days_slowDays with at least one slow run.
slow_runsSuccessful runs that took over 1.5x the job's usual time and 5+ minutes longer.
median_minutesThe middle run time of all its runs, in minutes.
avg_minutesThe average run time, in minutes.
max_minutesThe longest run, in minutes.
total_hoursAll its run time added up, in hours.
last_failureThe error code of its latest failed run (empty when none failed).
failing1 when the job had any failed run.
flaky1 when reliability is Flaky.
always_fails1 when reliability is Always fails.
top_by_timeThe job's name for the 10 jobs with the most run time; All others for the rest.
SQL with comments (86 lines)
-- Top 20 failed jobs. List price; dates from the Time Window.
-- Workspace id to name.
WITH ws AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, workspace_name
  FROM system.access.workspaces_latest
),
-- Job id to name: the latest row per job.
jb AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, CAST(job_id AS STRING) AS job_id, name
  FROM system.lakeflow.jobs
  QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY workspace_id, job_id ORDER BY change_time DESC) = 1
),
-- Job run rows that started in the window; a long run has one row per hour.
slices AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, CAST(job_id AS STRING) AS job_id, run_id,
         period_start_time, period_end_time, result_state, termination_code, run_duration_seconds
  FROM system.lakeflow.job_run_timeline
  WHERE period_start_time >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) AND period_start_time < date_add(:time_range.max, 1)
),
-- Each run's end row; a repaired run keeps its last attempt's result and error.
ends AS (
  -- a repaired run writes one end row per attempt: its result is the last one's
  SELECT workspace_id, job_id, run_id, period_end_time AS run_end, result_state, termination_code,
         NULLIF(run_duration_seconds, 0) AS reported_s
  FROM slices
  WHERE result_state IS NOT NULL
  QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY workspace_id, job_id, run_id ORDER BY period_end_time DESC) = 1
),
-- Each run's first row: when it started.
starts AS (
  SELECT workspace_id, job_id, run_id, MIN(period_start_time) AS run_start
  FROM slices
  GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id, run_id
),
-- One row per run: day, minutes it took, and 1 when it failed (failed, error or timed out).
runs AS (
  SELECT e.workspace_id, e.job_id, e.run_id, e.run_end, e.result_state, e.termination_code,
         CAST(s.run_start AS DATE) AS run_date,
         COALESCE(e.reported_s, timestampdiff(SECOND, s.run_start, e.run_end)) / 60.0 AS minutes,
         CASE WHEN e.result_state IN ('FAILED', 'ERROR', 'TIMED_OUT') THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS failed
  FROM ends e
  JOIN starts s ON s.workspace_id = e.workspace_id AND s.job_id = e.job_id AND s.run_id = e.run_id
),
-- Each job's usual run time: the median of its successful runs, once it has 5 or more.
usual AS (
  SELECT workspace_id, job_id, percentile(minutes, 0.5) AS usual_minutes
  FROM runs
  WHERE result_state = 'SUCCEEDED'
  GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id
  HAVING COUNT(*) >= 5
),
-- A slow run: succeeded, but took over 1.5x the job's usual time and 5+ minutes longer.
judged AS (
  SELECT r.*, u.usual_minutes,
         CASE WHEN r.result_state = 'SUCCEEDED' AND r.minutes > 1.5 * u.usual_minutes
                   AND r.minutes - u.usual_minutes >= 5 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS slow
  FROM runs r
  LEFT JOIN usual u ON u.workspace_id = r.workspace_id AND u.job_id = r.job_id
),
-- Per job: runs, failed and slow runs, days with a failure or a slow run, median, average and longest run, total hours, and the latest failure's error.
per_job AS (
  SELECT workspace_id, job_id, COUNT(*) AS runs, SUM(failed) AS failed_runs, SUM(slow) AS slow_runs,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN failed = 1 THEN run_date END) AS days_failed,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN slow = 1 THEN run_date END) AS days_slow,
         percentile(minutes, 0.5) AS median_minutes, AVG(minutes) AS avg_minutes, MAX(minutes) AS max_minutes,
         SUM(minutes) / 60.0 AS total_hours,
         -- the error of its latest failed run
         MAX_BY(termination_code, CASE WHEN failed = 1 THEN unix_timestamp(run_end) ELSE 0 END) AS last_code
  FROM judged
  GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id
),
-- Workspace and job names added.
named AS (
  SELECT j.*, COALESCE(ws.workspace_name, CAST(j.workspace_id AS STRING), 'Account-level') AS workspace, COALESCE(jb.name, j.job_id) AS job_name
  FROM per_job j
  LEFT JOIN ws ON ws.workspace_id = j.workspace_id
  LEFT JOIN jb ON jb.workspace_id = j.workspace_id AND jb.job_id = j.job_id
),
-- Reliability: Always fails (every run, 3+), Flaky (5+ runs, 20%+ failed), Some failures, No failures; ranked by failed runs and by total hours.
ranked AS (
  SELECT *, CASE WHEN failed_runs = 0 THEN 'No failures' WHEN failed_runs = runs AND runs >= 3 THEN 'Always fails' WHEN runs >= 5 AND 100.0 * failed_runs / runs >= 20 THEN 'Flaky' ELSE 'Some failures' END AS reliability,
         ROW_NUMBER() OVER (ORDER BY failed_runs DESC, failed_runs / runs DESC, workspace, job_name) AS fail_rank,
         ROW_NUMBER() OVER (ORDER BY total_hours DESC, workspace, job_name) AS time_rank
  FROM named
)
-- Final SELECT: the rows the widgets read.
SELECT concat(job_name, ' (', job_id, ')') AS job, workspace, reliability, 1 AS jobs,
       runs, failed_runs, ROUND(100.0 * failed_runs / runs, 1) AS failure_pct, days_failed, days_slow, slow_runs,
       ROUND(median_minutes, 1) AS median_minutes, ROUND(avg_minutes, 1) AS avg_minutes,
       ROUND(max_minutes, 1) AS max_minutes, ROUND(total_hours, 1) AS total_hours,
       CASE WHEN failed_runs > 0 THEN last_code END AS last_failure,
       CASE WHEN failed_runs > 0 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS failing,
       CASE WHEN reliability = 'Flaky' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS flaky,
       CASE WHEN reliability = 'Always fails' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS always_fails,
       CASE WHEN time_rank <= 10 THEN concat(job_name, ' · ', workspace) ELSE 'All others' END AS top_by_time
FROM ranked
WHERE failed_runs > 0 AND fail_rank <= 20
ORDER BY fail_rank

Failed job runs: $ of runs that failed, per job

Shows: Per job with failed or repaired runs: runs, failures, failure rate, the $ burned and the last error. Used by: The jobs half of Failed runs & queries.

Why it is built this way: The compute of a failed run is paid and delivers nothing; billing per run gives its $.

How its tables join

Grey: system tables. Blue border: a shared check (its steps are listed under Steps). White: steps of the query. Green: the rows the dashboard shows. ◆ marks the columns the joins use.

  1. lakeflow_failed_jobs_wasted_dbus → f: the check's rows
  2. jb → f: LEFT JOIN ON workspace_id, job_id: names
  3. ws → f: LEFT JOIN ON workspace_id: names

1. System tables it reads

  • system.access.workspaces_latest
  • system.billing.list_prices
  • system.billing.usage
  • system.lakeflow.job_run_timeline
  • system.lakeflow.jobs

2. Steps (CTEs), in order

  1. failed_jobs: The shared check lakeflow_failed_jobs_wasted_dbus. Failed job runs: per job, the $ of runs that failed, plus the failed part of runs that were repaired.
    1. price: List price of every SKU over time, with the date each price really ended.
    2. usage_rows: Every billed DBU row of a job in the window, priced at list.
    3. job_usage: Per job: total DBUs and $, and how much billing has no run id.
    4. run_usage: Per job run: DBUs and $.
    5. cand: Job runs that ended in the window.
    6. run_rows: Every time slice of those runs.
    7. slices: Attempts numbered within each run: a repair starts a new attempt.
    8. attempts: One row per attempt: how long it ran and how it ended.
    9. att2: Each run's last attempt number added.
    10. runs: Per run: its final result, total seconds, and seconds in attempts that failed before a repair.
    11. classed: Each run labelled failed, cancelled, repaired (failed, then fixed) or ok, with its $.
    12. last_fail: Each job's most recent failure and its error code.
    13. per_job: Per job: runs, failures, repairs; $ of failed runs plus the failed share of repaired runs.
    14. judged: The job's billing, failure rate and wasted $ added; why one can't be priced.
    Its SQL (243 lines)
    -- Failed job runs: $ of runs that failed, per job. List price; dates from the Time Window.
-- The shared check lakeflow_failed_jobs_wasted_dbus. Failed job runs: per job, the $ of runs that failed, plus the failed part of runs that were repaired.
failed_jobs AS (
  SELECT datediff(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), :time_range.min) AS window_days, q.*
  FROM (
  -- List price of every SKU over time, with the date each price really ended.
  WITH price AS (
    SELECT sku_name, cloud, usage_unit, price_start_time, price_end_time,
           CAST(pricing.effective_list.default AS DOUBLE) AS list_rate
    FROM 
  (
      SELECT account_id, sku_name, cloud, currency_code, usage_unit, pricing,
             price_start_time, effective_end AS price_end_time
      FROM (
          SELECT account_id, sku_name, cloud, currency_code, usage_unit, pricing,
                 price_start_time, price_end_time, next_start_time,
                 -- CASE, not LEAST, so a NULL end/next behaves the same on DuckDB and Databricks.
                 CASE
                     WHEN price_end_time IS NULL THEN next_start_time
                     WHEN next_start_time IS NULL THEN price_end_time
                     WHEN price_end_time <= next_start_time THEN price_end_time
                     ELSE next_start_time
                 END AS effective_end
          FROM (
              SELECT account_id, sku_name, cloud, currency_code, usage_unit, pricing,
                     price_start_time, price_end_time,
                     LEAD(price_start_time) OVER (
                         PARTITION BY sku_name, cloud, usage_unit
                         ORDER BY price_start_time, price_end_time NULLS LAST
                     ) AS next_start_time
              FROM `system`.`billing`.`list_prices`
              WHERE currency_code = 'USD'
          ) ranked
      ) capped
      WHERE effective_end IS NULL OR effective_end > price_start_time
  )
   list_prices
  ),
  -- Every billed DBU row of a job in the window, priced at list.
  usage_rows AS (
    SELECT u.workspace_id,
           u.usage_metadata.job_id     AS job_id,
           u.usage_metadata.job_run_id AS job_run_id,
           u.usage_quantity            AS dbus,
           u.usage_quantity * p.list_rate AS usd,
           CASE WHEN p.list_rate IS NULL AND upper(u.sku_name) NOT LIKE '%FREE_USAGE%'
                THEN u.usage_quantity ELSE 0 END AS unpriced_q,
           CASE WHEN p.list_rate IS NOT NULL THEN u.usage_quantity ELSE 0 END AS priced_q
    FROM `system`.`billing`.`usage` u
    LEFT JOIN price p
      ON  u.sku_name = p.sku_name AND u.cloud = p.cloud AND u.usage_unit = p.usage_unit
      AND u.usage_end_time >= p.price_start_time
      AND (p.price_end_time IS NULL OR u.usage_end_time < p.price_end_time)
    WHERE u.usage_date >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE)
      AND u.usage_date < date_add(:time_range.max, 1)
      AND upper(u.usage_unit) = 'DBU'
      AND u.usage_metadata.job_id IS NOT NULL
  ),
  -- Per job: total DBUs and $, and how much billing has no run id.
  job_usage AS (
    SELECT workspace_id, job_id,
           SUM(dbus) AS net_dbus, SUM(usd) AS usd_priced_part,
           SUM(unpriced_q) AS unpriced_q, SUM(priced_q) AS priced_q,
           SUM(CASE WHEN job_run_id IS NULL THEN dbus ELSE 0 END)       AS unattributed_dbus,
           SUM(CASE WHEN job_run_id IS NULL THEN usd END)               AS unattributed_usd,
           SUM(CASE WHEN job_run_id IS NULL THEN unpriced_q ELSE 0 END) AS unattributed_unpriced_q,
           SUM(CASE WHEN job_run_id IS NOT NULL THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)      AS run_rows
    FROM usage_rows
    GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id
  ),
  -- Per job run: DBUs and $.
  run_usage AS (
    SELECT workspace_id, job_id, job_run_id,
           SUM(dbus) AS run_dbus, SUM(usd) AS run_usd, SUM(unpriced_q) AS run_unpriced_q
    FROM usage_rows
    WHERE job_run_id IS NOT NULL
    GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id, job_run_id
  ),
  -- Job runs that ended in the window.
  cand AS (
    SELECT DISTINCT workspace_id, job_id, run_id
    FROM `system`.`lakeflow`.`job_run_timeline`
    WHERE period_start_time >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE)
      AND period_end_time < CAST(date_add(:time_range.max, 1) AS TIMESTAMP)
      AND result_state IS NOT NULL
  ),
  -- Every time slice of those runs.
  run_rows AS (
    SELECT t.workspace_id, t.job_id, t.run_id, t.period_start_time, t.period_end_time,
           t.result_state, t.termination_code,
           unix_timestamp(t.period_end_time) - unix_timestamp(t.period_start_time) AS slice_s
    FROM `system`.`lakeflow`.`job_run_timeline` t
    JOIN cand c ON c.workspace_id = t.workspace_id AND c.job_id = t.job_id AND c.run_id = t.run_id
  ),
  -- Attempts numbered within each run: a repair starts a new attempt.
  slices AS (
    SELECT r.*,
           1 + COALESCE(SUM(CASE WHEN r.result_state IS NOT NULL THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) OVER (
                 PARTITION BY r.workspace_id, r.job_id, r.run_id
                 ORDER BY r.period_end_time, r.period_start_time
                 ROWS BETWEEN UNBOUNDED PRECEDING AND 1 PRECEDING), 0) AS attempt_no
    FROM run_rows r
  ),
  -- One row per attempt: how long it ran and how it ended.
  attempts AS (
    SELECT workspace_id, job_id, run_id, attempt_no,
           SUM(slice_s)          AS attempt_s,
           MAX(result_state)     AS attempt_state,   -- one end row per attempt, or none (in flight)
           MAX(termination_code) AS attempt_code,
           MAX(period_end_time)  AS attempt_end
    FROM slices
    GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id, run_id, attempt_no
  ),
  -- Each run's last attempt number added.
  att2 AS (
    SELECT a.*, MAX(a.attempt_no) OVER (PARTITION BY a.workspace_id, a.job_id, a.run_id) AS last_attempt
    FROM attempts a
  ),
  -- Per run: its final result, total seconds, and seconds in attempts that failed before a repair.
  runs AS (
    SELECT workspace_id, job_id, run_id,
           MAX(CASE WHEN attempt_no = last_attempt THEN attempt_state END) AS final_state,
           MAX(CASE WHEN attempt_no = last_attempt THEN attempt_end END)   AS final_end,
           MAX(CASE WHEN attempt_no = last_attempt AND attempt_state IS NULL THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS in_flight,
           SUM(attempt_s) AS run_s,
           SUM(CASE WHEN attempt_no < last_attempt AND attempt_state IN ('FAILED', 'ERROR', 'TIMED_OUT')
                    THEN attempt_s ELSE 0 END) AS failed_attempt_s
    FROM att2
    GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id, run_id
  ),
  -- Each run labelled failed, cancelled, repaired (failed, then fixed) or ok, with its $.
  classed AS (
    SELECT r.*, ru.run_dbus, ru.run_usd, ru.run_unpriced_q,
           CASE WHEN r.final_state IN ('FAILED', 'ERROR', 'TIMED_OUT') THEN 'failed'
                WHEN r.final_state = 'CANCELLED' THEN 'cancelled'
                WHEN r.failed_attempt_s > 0 THEN 'repaired'
                ELSE 'ok' END AS run_class
    FROM runs r
    LEFT JOIN run_usage ru
      ON ru.workspace_id = r.workspace_id AND ru.job_id = r.job_id AND ru.job_run_id = r.run_id
    WHERE r.in_flight = 0
      AND r.final_end < CAST(date_add(:time_range.max, 1) AS TIMESTAMP)
  ),
  -- Each job's most recent failure and its error code.
  last_fail AS (
    -- the most recent failed attempt (a final failure or a repaired one) of these runs
    SELECT a.workspace_id, a.job_id, a.run_id AS last_failed_run_id,
           a.attempt_code AS last_failed_termination_code
    FROM att2 a
    JOIN classed c ON c.workspace_id = a.workspace_id AND c.job_id = a.job_id AND c.run_id = a.run_id
    WHERE a.attempt_state IN ('FAILED', 'ERROR', 'TIMED_OUT')
    QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY a.workspace_id, a.job_id
                               ORDER BY a.attempt_end DESC, a.run_id DESC) = 1
  ),
  -- Per job: runs, failures, repairs; $ of failed runs plus the failed share of repaired runs.
  per_job AS (
    SELECT workspace_id, job_id,
           COUNT(*) AS distinct_runs,
           SUM(CASE WHEN run_class = 'failed'    THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS failed_runs,
           SUM(CASE WHEN run_class = 'repaired'  THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS repaired_runs,
           SUM(CASE WHEN run_class = 'cancelled' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS cancelled_runs,
           SUM(CASE WHEN run_class = 'failed' AND run_dbus IS NULL THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS failed_runs_unbilled,
           SUM(CASE WHEN run_class = 'failed' THEN run_dbus
                    WHEN run_class = 'repaired' AND run_s > 0 THEN run_dbus * failed_attempt_s / run_s END) AS wasted_dbus_raw,
           SUM(CASE WHEN run_class = 'failed' THEN run_usd END) AS failed_usd,
           SUM(CASE WHEN run_class = 'repaired' AND run_s > 0 THEN run_usd * failed_attempt_s / run_s END) AS repair_usd,
           SUM(CASE WHEN run_class = 'cancelled' THEN run_usd END) AS cancelled_usd,
           SUM(CASE WHEN run_class IN ('failed', 'repaired') THEN COALESCE(run_unpriced_q, 0) ELSE 0 END) AS waste_unpriced_q
    FROM classed
    GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id
  ),
  -- The job's billing, failure rate and wasted $ added; why one can't be priced.
  judged AS (
    SELECT p.*, j.net_dbus, j.usd_priced_part, j.unpriced_q, j.priced_q, j.unattributed_dbus,
           j.unattributed_usd, j.unattributed_unpriced_q,
           lf.last_failed_run_id, lf.last_failed_termination_code,
           (j.workspace_id IS NOT NULL) AS has_usage,
           p.failed_runs * 100.0 / p.distinct_runs AS rate_raw,
           COALESCE(p.failed_usd, 0) + COALESCE(p.repair_usd, 0) AS waste_raw,
           CASE WHEN j.workspace_id IS NULL THEN 'no_billing_rows'
                WHEN j.run_rows = 0          THEN 'no_run_id_in_billing'
                WHEN p.waste_unpriced_q > 0  THEN 'unpriced'
           END AS na_reason
    FROM per_job p
    LEFT JOIN job_usage j  ON j.workspace_id = p.workspace_id  AND j.job_id = p.job_id
    LEFT JOIN last_fail lf ON lf.workspace_id = p.workspace_id AND lf.job_id = p.job_id
    WHERE p.failed_runs > 0 OR p.repaired_runs > 0
  )
  SELECT workspace_id, job_id,
         distinct_runs, failed_runs, repaired_runs, cancelled_runs, failed_runs_unbilled,
         ROUND(rate_raw, 1)                                                     AS failure_rate_pct,
         last_failed_termination_code, last_failed_run_id,
         CASE WHEN na_reason IN ('no_billing_rows', 'no_run_id_in_billing') THEN NULL
              ELSE ROUND(COALESCE(wasted_dbus_raw, 0), 2) END                   AS wasted_dbus,
         CASE WHEN na_reason IS NULL THEN ROUND(COALESCE(failed_usd, 0), 2) END    AS est_failed_usd_list,
         CASE WHEN na_reason IS NULL THEN ROUND(COALESCE(repair_usd, 0), 2) END    AS est_repair_usd_list,
         CASE WHEN na_reason IS NULL THEN ROUND(waste_raw, 2) END                   AS est_wasted_usd_list,
         CASE WHEN na_reason IS NULL THEN ROUND(COALESCE(cancelled_usd, 0), 2) END AS est_cancelled_usd_list,
         ROUND(net_dbus, 2)                                                     AS net_dbus,
         CASE WHEN NOT has_usage OR unpriced_q > 0 THEN NULL
              ELSE ROUND(COALESCE(usd_priced_part, 0), 2) END                   AS est_usd_list,
         CASE WHEN NOT has_usage THEN NULL
              WHEN unattributed_dbus = 0 THEN 0
              WHEN unattributed_unpriced_q > 0 THEN NULL
              ELSE ROUND(unattributed_usd, 2) END                               AS est_unattributed_usd_list,
         CASE WHEN na_reason IS NULL AND NOT (unpriced_q > 0)
              THEN ROUND(waste_raw * 100.0 / NULLIF(usd_priced_part, 0), 1) END AS wasted_share_pct,
         CASE WHEN NOT has_usage THEN NULL
              WHEN unpriced_q > 0 THEN 'unpriced' WHEN priced_q = 0 THEN 'free' ELSE 'priced' END AS price_basis,
         CASE
           WHEN na_reason = 'no_billing_rows' THEN
             'failed runs or failed attempts found, but no billed usage for this job in the window - the runs may have failed before compute started, or billing has not landed yet'
           WHEN na_reason = 'no_run_id_in_billing' THEN
             'failed runs or failed attempts found, but this job''s billing rows carry no run id, so what they cost cannot be measured (the job''s whole spend is in est_usd_list, not spread over its runs)'
           WHEN na_reason = 'unpriced' THEN
             'the failed runs'' usage has no list price, so there is no dollar figure (wasted_dbus is shown)'
           ELSE
             CONCAT_WS('; ',
               CONCAT(failed_runs, ' of ', distinct_runs, ' runs failed (', CAST(ROUND(rate_raw, 0) AS BIGINT), '%)'),
               CASE WHEN repaired_runs = 1 THEN '1 run failed first and was repaired'
                    WHEN repaired_runs > 1 THEN CONCAT(repaired_runs, ' runs failed first and were repaired') END,
               CASE WHEN failed_runs_unbilled = 1 THEN '1 failed run had no billed usage'
                    WHEN failed_runs_unbilled > 1 THEN CONCAT(failed_runs_unbilled, ' failed runs had no billed usage') END,
               CASE WHEN last_failed_termination_code IS NOT NULL
                    THEN CONCAT('last failure ', last_failed_termination_code) END)
         END                                                                    AS waste_reason,
         na_reason                                                              AS not_assessed_reason,
         -- the $ floor (1) gates only the $ branches, never the failure-rate ones -- a
         -- job failing outright must not read OK just because it is cheap. The rate branches
         -- themselves need 3 runs first -- 1 of 1 failed is not a rate, just one data point.
         CASE
           WHEN na_reason IS NOT NULL                                                        THEN 'NOT_ASSESSED'
           WHEN (distinct_runs >= 3 AND rate_raw >= 50)
             OR waste_raw >= GREATEST(200, 1)                       THEN 'CRITICAL'
           WHEN (distinct_runs >= 3 AND rate_raw >= 10)
             OR waste_raw >= GREATEST(20, 1)                       THEN 'WARN'
           ELSE 'OK'
         END AS status
  FROM judged
  ORDER BY CASE status WHEN 'CRITICAL' THEN 0 WHEN 'WARN' THEN 1 WHEN 'NOT_ASSESSED' THEN 2 ELSE 3 END,
           est_wasted_usd_list DESC NULLS LAST, failed_runs DESC, workspace_id, job_id
  ) q
)
  2. ws: Workspace id to name.
    Its SQL (5 lines)
    -- Workspace id to name.
ws AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, workspace_name
  FROM system.access.workspaces_latest
)
  3. jb: Job id to name: the latest row per job.
    Its SQL (6 lines)
    -- Job id to name: the latest row per job.
jb AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, CAST(job_id AS STRING) AS job_id, name
  FROM system.lakeflow.jobs
  QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY workspace_id, job_id ORDER BY change_time DESC) = 1
)
  4. f: Workspace and job names added; ranked by wasted $ for the top 10.
    Its SQL (8 lines)
    -- Workspace and job names added; ranked by wasted $ for the top 10.
f AS (
  SELECT f.*, COALESCE(ws.workspace_name, CAST(f.workspace_id AS STRING), 'Account-level') AS workspace, COALESCE(jb.name, CAST(f.job_id AS STRING)) AS job_name,
         ROW_NUMBER() OVER (ORDER BY f.est_wasted_usd_list DESC NULLS LAST, f.failed_runs DESC, f.job_id) AS rn
  FROM failed_jobs f
  LEFT JOIN ws ON ws.workspace_id = CAST(f.workspace_id AS STRING)
  LEFT JOIN jb ON jb.workspace_id = CAST(f.workspace_id AS STRING) AND jb.job_id = CAST(f.job_id AS STRING)
)

3. Final query

One row per job with failures; the 10 costliest keep their name for the chart.

4. Columns it returns

ColumnMeaning
jobThe job's name and id, as name (id). One-off submitted runs have no name and show the id.
workspaceThe workspace's name (its id when the name is unknown).
statusCRITICAL, WARN, OK or NOT_ASSESSED (not enough data to judge), from the rule in Rules.
top_jobThe job's name for the 10 with the most wasted $; All others for the rest.
runsDistinct runs in the window.
failed_runsRuns that failed.
repaired_runsRuns that were repaired (re-run after a failure).
failure_rate_pctfailed_runs / runs x 100.
wasted_usdList-price $ of the failed runs and the failed attempts of repaired runs.
last_failureThe error code of the latest failed run.
flagged1 when status is CRITICAL or WARN, else 0; tiles add it up to count flagged rows.
SQL with comments (251 lines)
-- Failed job runs: $ of runs that failed, per job. List price; dates from the Time Window.
-- The shared check lakeflow_failed_jobs_wasted_dbus. Failed job runs: per job, the $ of runs that failed, plus the failed part of runs that were repaired.
WITH failed_jobs AS (
  SELECT datediff(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), :time_range.min) AS window_days, q.*
  FROM (
  -- List price of every SKU over time, with the date each price really ended.
  WITH price AS (
    SELECT sku_name, cloud, usage_unit, price_start_time, price_end_time,
           CAST(pricing.effective_list.default AS DOUBLE) AS list_rate
    FROM 
  (
      SELECT account_id, sku_name, cloud, currency_code, usage_unit, pricing,
             price_start_time, effective_end AS price_end_time
      FROM (
          SELECT account_id, sku_name, cloud, currency_code, usage_unit, pricing,
                 price_start_time, price_end_time, next_start_time,
                 -- CASE, not LEAST, so a NULL end/next behaves the same on DuckDB and Databricks.
                 CASE
                     WHEN price_end_time IS NULL THEN next_start_time
                     WHEN next_start_time IS NULL THEN price_end_time
                     WHEN price_end_time <= next_start_time THEN price_end_time
                     ELSE next_start_time
                 END AS effective_end
          FROM (
              SELECT account_id, sku_name, cloud, currency_code, usage_unit, pricing,
                     price_start_time, price_end_time,
                     LEAD(price_start_time) OVER (
                         PARTITION BY sku_name, cloud, usage_unit
                         ORDER BY price_start_time, price_end_time NULLS LAST
                     ) AS next_start_time
              FROM `system`.`billing`.`list_prices`
              WHERE currency_code = 'USD'
          ) ranked
      ) capped
      WHERE effective_end IS NULL OR effective_end > price_start_time
  )
   list_prices
  ),
  -- Every billed DBU row of a job in the window, priced at list.
  usage_rows AS (
    SELECT u.workspace_id,
           u.usage_metadata.job_id     AS job_id,
           u.usage_metadata.job_run_id AS job_run_id,
           u.usage_quantity            AS dbus,
           u.usage_quantity * p.list_rate AS usd,
           CASE WHEN p.list_rate IS NULL AND upper(u.sku_name) NOT LIKE '%FREE_USAGE%'
                THEN u.usage_quantity ELSE 0 END AS unpriced_q,
           CASE WHEN p.list_rate IS NOT NULL THEN u.usage_quantity ELSE 0 END AS priced_q
    FROM `system`.`billing`.`usage` u
    LEFT JOIN price p
      ON  u.sku_name = p.sku_name AND u.cloud = p.cloud AND u.usage_unit = p.usage_unit
      AND u.usage_end_time >= p.price_start_time
      AND (p.price_end_time IS NULL OR u.usage_end_time < p.price_end_time)
    WHERE u.usage_date >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE)
      AND u.usage_date < date_add(:time_range.max, 1)
      AND upper(u.usage_unit) = 'DBU'
      AND u.usage_metadata.job_id IS NOT NULL
  ),
  -- Per job: total DBUs and $, and how much billing has no run id.
  job_usage AS (
    SELECT workspace_id, job_id,
           SUM(dbus) AS net_dbus, SUM(usd) AS usd_priced_part,
           SUM(unpriced_q) AS unpriced_q, SUM(priced_q) AS priced_q,
           SUM(CASE WHEN job_run_id IS NULL THEN dbus ELSE 0 END)       AS unattributed_dbus,
           SUM(CASE WHEN job_run_id IS NULL THEN usd END)               AS unattributed_usd,
           SUM(CASE WHEN job_run_id IS NULL THEN unpriced_q ELSE 0 END) AS unattributed_unpriced_q,
           SUM(CASE WHEN job_run_id IS NOT NULL THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)      AS run_rows
    FROM usage_rows
    GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id
  ),
  -- Per job run: DBUs and $.
  run_usage AS (
    SELECT workspace_id, job_id, job_run_id,
           SUM(dbus) AS run_dbus, SUM(usd) AS run_usd, SUM(unpriced_q) AS run_unpriced_q
    FROM usage_rows
    WHERE job_run_id IS NOT NULL
    GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id, job_run_id
  ),
  -- Job runs that ended in the window.
  cand AS (
    SELECT DISTINCT workspace_id, job_id, run_id
    FROM `system`.`lakeflow`.`job_run_timeline`
    WHERE period_start_time >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE)
      AND period_end_time < CAST(date_add(:time_range.max, 1) AS TIMESTAMP)
      AND result_state IS NOT NULL
  ),
  -- Every time slice of those runs.
  run_rows AS (
    SELECT t.workspace_id, t.job_id, t.run_id, t.period_start_time, t.period_end_time,
           t.result_state, t.termination_code,
           unix_timestamp(t.period_end_time) - unix_timestamp(t.period_start_time) AS slice_s
    FROM `system`.`lakeflow`.`job_run_timeline` t
    JOIN cand c ON c.workspace_id = t.workspace_id AND c.job_id = t.job_id AND c.run_id = t.run_id
  ),
  -- Attempts numbered within each run: a repair starts a new attempt.
  slices AS (
    SELECT r.*,
           1 + COALESCE(SUM(CASE WHEN r.result_state IS NOT NULL THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) OVER (
                 PARTITION BY r.workspace_id, r.job_id, r.run_id
                 ORDER BY r.period_end_time, r.period_start_time
                 ROWS BETWEEN UNBOUNDED PRECEDING AND 1 PRECEDING), 0) AS attempt_no
    FROM run_rows r
  ),
  -- One row per attempt: how long it ran and how it ended.
  attempts AS (
    SELECT workspace_id, job_id, run_id, attempt_no,
           SUM(slice_s)          AS attempt_s,
           MAX(result_state)     AS attempt_state,   -- one end row per attempt, or none (in flight)
           MAX(termination_code) AS attempt_code,
           MAX(period_end_time)  AS attempt_end
    FROM slices
    GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id, run_id, attempt_no
  ),
  -- Each run's last attempt number added.
  att2 AS (
    SELECT a.*, MAX(a.attempt_no) OVER (PARTITION BY a.workspace_id, a.job_id, a.run_id) AS last_attempt
    FROM attempts a
  ),
  -- Per run: its final result, total seconds, and seconds in attempts that failed before a repair.
  runs AS (
    SELECT workspace_id, job_id, run_id,
           MAX(CASE WHEN attempt_no = last_attempt THEN attempt_state END) AS final_state,
           MAX(CASE WHEN attempt_no = last_attempt THEN attempt_end END)   AS final_end,
           MAX(CASE WHEN attempt_no = last_attempt AND attempt_state IS NULL THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS in_flight,
           SUM(attempt_s) AS run_s,
           SUM(CASE WHEN attempt_no < last_attempt AND attempt_state IN ('FAILED', 'ERROR', 'TIMED_OUT')
                    THEN attempt_s ELSE 0 END) AS failed_attempt_s
    FROM att2
    GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id, run_id
  ),
  -- Each run labelled failed, cancelled, repaired (failed, then fixed) or ok, with its $.
  classed AS (
    SELECT r.*, ru.run_dbus, ru.run_usd, ru.run_unpriced_q,
           CASE WHEN r.final_state IN ('FAILED', 'ERROR', 'TIMED_OUT') THEN 'failed'
                WHEN r.final_state = 'CANCELLED' THEN 'cancelled'
                WHEN r.failed_attempt_s > 0 THEN 'repaired'
                ELSE 'ok' END AS run_class
    FROM runs r
    LEFT JOIN run_usage ru
      ON ru.workspace_id = r.workspace_id AND ru.job_id = r.job_id AND ru.job_run_id = r.run_id
    WHERE r.in_flight = 0
      AND r.final_end < CAST(date_add(:time_range.max, 1) AS TIMESTAMP)
  ),
  -- Each job's most recent failure and its error code.
  last_fail AS (
    -- the most recent failed attempt (a final failure or a repaired one) of these runs
    SELECT a.workspace_id, a.job_id, a.run_id AS last_failed_run_id,
           a.attempt_code AS last_failed_termination_code
    FROM att2 a
    JOIN classed c ON c.workspace_id = a.workspace_id AND c.job_id = a.job_id AND c.run_id = a.run_id
    WHERE a.attempt_state IN ('FAILED', 'ERROR', 'TIMED_OUT')
    QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY a.workspace_id, a.job_id
                               ORDER BY a.attempt_end DESC, a.run_id DESC) = 1
  ),
  -- Per job: runs, failures, repairs; $ of failed runs plus the failed share of repaired runs.
  per_job AS (
    SELECT workspace_id, job_id,
           COUNT(*) AS distinct_runs,
           SUM(CASE WHEN run_class = 'failed'    THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS failed_runs,
           SUM(CASE WHEN run_class = 'repaired'  THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS repaired_runs,
           SUM(CASE WHEN run_class = 'cancelled' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS cancelled_runs,
           SUM(CASE WHEN run_class = 'failed' AND run_dbus IS NULL THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS failed_runs_unbilled,
           SUM(CASE WHEN run_class = 'failed' THEN run_dbus
                    WHEN run_class = 'repaired' AND run_s > 0 THEN run_dbus * failed_attempt_s / run_s END) AS wasted_dbus_raw,
           SUM(CASE WHEN run_class = 'failed' THEN run_usd END) AS failed_usd,
           SUM(CASE WHEN run_class = 'repaired' AND run_s > 0 THEN run_usd * failed_attempt_s / run_s END) AS repair_usd,
           SUM(CASE WHEN run_class = 'cancelled' THEN run_usd END) AS cancelled_usd,
           SUM(CASE WHEN run_class IN ('failed', 'repaired') THEN COALESCE(run_unpriced_q, 0) ELSE 0 END) AS waste_unpriced_q
    FROM classed
    GROUP BY workspace_id, job_id
  ),
  -- The job's billing, failure rate and wasted $ added; why one can't be priced.
  judged AS (
    SELECT p.*, j.net_dbus, j.usd_priced_part, j.unpriced_q, j.priced_q, j.unattributed_dbus,
           j.unattributed_usd, j.unattributed_unpriced_q,
           lf.last_failed_run_id, lf.last_failed_termination_code,
           (j.workspace_id IS NOT NULL) AS has_usage,
           p.failed_runs * 100.0 / p.distinct_runs AS rate_raw,
           COALESCE(p.failed_usd, 0) + COALESCE(p.repair_usd, 0) AS waste_raw,
           CASE WHEN j.workspace_id IS NULL THEN 'no_billing_rows'
                WHEN j.run_rows = 0          THEN 'no_run_id_in_billing'
                WHEN p.waste_unpriced_q > 0  THEN 'unpriced'
           END AS na_reason
    FROM per_job p
    LEFT JOIN job_usage j  ON j.workspace_id = p.workspace_id  AND j.job_id = p.job_id
    LEFT JOIN last_fail lf ON lf.workspace_id = p.workspace_id AND lf.job_id = p.job_id
    WHERE p.failed_runs > 0 OR p.repaired_runs > 0
  )
  SELECT workspace_id, job_id,
         distinct_runs, failed_runs, repaired_runs, cancelled_runs, failed_runs_unbilled,
         ROUND(rate_raw, 1)                                                     AS failure_rate_pct,
         last_failed_termination_code, last_failed_run_id,
         CASE WHEN na_reason IN ('no_billing_rows', 'no_run_id_in_billing') THEN NULL
              ELSE ROUND(COALESCE(wasted_dbus_raw, 0), 2) END                   AS wasted_dbus,
         CASE WHEN na_reason IS NULL THEN ROUND(COALESCE(failed_usd, 0), 2) END    AS est_failed_usd_list,
         CASE WHEN na_reason IS NULL THEN ROUND(COALESCE(repair_usd, 0), 2) END    AS est_repair_usd_list,
         CASE WHEN na_reason IS NULL THEN ROUND(waste_raw, 2) END                   AS est_wasted_usd_list,
         CASE WHEN na_reason IS NULL THEN ROUND(COALESCE(cancelled_usd, 0), 2) END AS est_cancelled_usd_list,
         ROUND(net_dbus, 2)                                                     AS net_dbus,
         CASE WHEN NOT has_usage OR unpriced_q > 0 THEN NULL
              ELSE ROUND(COALESCE(usd_priced_part, 0), 2) END                   AS est_usd_list,
